    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/z-galaxy/zbus/releases">zbus's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>zbus-5.19.0</h2>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>✨ Add Builder::async_io_unix_stream and async_io_tcp_stream.</li>
    # <li>✨ Make the tokio and async-io features additive.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Changed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>♻️ Remove unnecessary clones.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Deprecated</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>🗑️ Deprecate unix_stream/tcp_stream when tokio is disabled.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Documentation</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>📝 Document the tokio and async-io runtime selection.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>🥅 throw Error::Connection on connection failure.</li>
    # <li>🥅 Add new error variant Error::Connection.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Other</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>✏️ Fix a typo in the executor docs.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Testing</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>✅ Test runtime selection with both backends enabled.</li>
    # <li>✅ add tests for Error::Connection.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>zbus_macros-5.19.0</h2>

    ^ /!\ contains WebIDL changes! did we ship a new API?
    # Adds support for eager evaluation, with the console evaluating code without side-effects as the user is typing. In eager mode, evaluation is aborted upon hitting effectful bytecode or conditionally upon native calls.
    # 
    # Known issues:
    # - Calls to native code are currently assumed to always have side-effects (and therefore aborted), see the `eagerDbg.onNativeCall` hook
    # - Firefox eagerly renders `SyntaxError`. Nominally these would be filtered out on the client, but this filter relies upon the `error` preview, which we don't support yet. This means that you get shown syntax errors as you type
    # 
    # Testing: Covered by new python tests
    # Fixes: Part of #39858

    # This fixes a potential bug if the memory cache is small.
    # Requests that are still ongoing, get inserted into the cache, even when they do not have yet a finished body.

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47692	(@mrobinson, #47692)	script: Run live range update steps on complete move subtree (#47692)
    ^ commit e98cb7c0da486845a8d879ccdbe16d34851ff482
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # The specification says that live range updates steps need to run on the
    # entirely (shadow-exclusive) subtree of a moved node.  Because the moving
    # steps are only run on the moved node itself (not the subtree), this wasn't
    # happening properly.
    # 
    # This makes move more expensive, but is essential for correctness. The real
    # fix for the performance regression is to move live ranges to be stored globally
    # on the `Document` and only run the subtree steps if there is a relevant live
    # range or a relevant `Selection`.
    # 
    # Testing: This causes a WPT test to pass.
    # 
https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47686	(@mxmgorin, #47686)	script: Do not panic when a memory report outlives its event loop (#47686)
    ^ commit d078b2cab4f0ac68d8fa981a1d7faf1092e959c4
    # `ScriptEventLoopSender` panics if a memory profiler callback tries to send to an event loop that has already shut down:
    # 
    # ```
    # thread 'MemoryProfiler' panicked at
    # components/script/messaging.rs:372:28:
    # called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: "SendError(..)"
    # ```
    # 
    # The other `OpaqueSender` implementations already handle send failures by logging a warning. This makes `ScriptEventLoopSender` consistent with them and avoids taking down the process-wide profiler.
    # 
    # **Testing:** No automated test. The issue does reproduce in an embedder that requests a memory report once a second, against a page that spawns a worker every 15 ms and terminates it 30 ms later.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47545	(@kkoyung, #47545)	script: Expand `supports()` validation coverage in WebCrypto (#47545)
    dom; webcrypto bugfix
    ^ commit 47f0cd3297539824e5ac711e43c28875440decfc
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # WebCrypto API specification has updated some cryptography operations in order to expand `supports()` validation coverage. We follow the updates.
    # 
    # The update basically consists of moving all parameter validation to the beginning of the operations, and adding some new parameter validation which were done implicitly. Therefore, these operations should not have observable changes from user perspective.
    # 
    # Meanwhile, since the actually implementation of Step 8 of the **check support for an algorithm** algorithm is based on how these operations are written, and it follows the above changes, the `supports()` method, which relies on the **check support for an algorithm** algorithm, improves its validation coverage.
    # 
    # We also update our comment next to Step 8 based on the discussion in https://github.com/WICG/webcrypto-modern-algos/pull/74.
    # 
    # Spec Update: https://github.com/w3c/webcrypto/pull/558
    # WPT Update: https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/pull/62115
    # 
    # Testing: Pass more WPT tests.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47682	(@jdm, #47682)	script: Start replacing universal `Rc<Promise>` with explicitly rooted/traced types (#47682)
    dom; part of https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/39139
    ^ commit 463b9e44401760a83f1151e0da5f18ff0c0287d6
    # These changes introduce `PromiseRoot` and `TracedPromise` types which wrap the existing `Rc<Promise>`. The eventual goal is to ensure that all script/ code uses these types appropriately so we stop storing permanently-rooted promise objects inside of traced Rust types on the heap. By introducing the types without any behaviour change we make it easier to incrementally migrate existing code.

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47478	(@mrobinson, #47478)	script: No longer store `Selection` range as a live range (#47478)
    ^ commit 081f1396cc8e0373c1d4095d2b1421458ede50bd
    # Instead of storing the `Selection` as a live range, store it as a new
    # range type. This is necessary so that, in a followup change, selections
    # can span multiple shadow trees (necessary according to the
    # specification). This requires duplicating a good deal of the live range
    # update machinery, but this duplication mirrors what other browsers do:
    # 
    # 1. Gecko keeps a composed range as a member of a selection live range
    #    and adjusts them separately.
    # 1. Chrome keeps potentially-cross-root boundaries separately, as we do,
    #    and then keeps a cache of a mirrored live range.
    # 
    # Testing: This should not change observable behavior so is covered by existing tests.
    # Fixes: This is part of #7492.

    ^ commit c91fc1729a6d27504c98d84a8b954f7c3f2cc0ec
    # As a cleanup follow-up to the migration to pass `&mut JSContext`, this PR is the result of applying the following regex replacement to the codebase:
    # 
    # Before:
    # 
    # ```
    # reflect_dom_object_with_cx\((([^<]|\n)+),\n\s+?cx,\n\s+?\)\n
    # ```

    # After:
    # 
    # ```
    # reflect_dom_object(cx, $1)\n
    # ```

    # This did not catch all usages, but is a good start.

    # Part of #40600

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47632	(@mrobinson, @jdm, #47632)	script: Implement the `WindowProxy` `ProxyTraps` according to specification (#47632)
    ^ commit dfac8fae407ab2447de171be7a5fa7d108ae5016
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    ^ /!\ contains WebIDL changes! did we ship a new API?
    # This change re-implements the `ProxyTraps` for `WindowProxy` according
    # to the specification which says that they should always take into
    # account same origin concerns. In addition to fixing security and
    # correctness issues, this means that now there is only a single type of
    # `WindowProxy` `ProxyTrap` instead of two, simplifying the code.
    # 
    # This change exposes quite a few places that we should be inheriting
    # the `Origin`, but are not properly doing so. #47630 is an example
    # of a fix necessary to get more of these tests passing.
    # 
    # Testing: This gets many tests passing and reveal quite a few new
    # fails that are progressions.
    # Fixes: #28556.
    # Fixes: #44669.
    # Fixes: #47548.
    # 
https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47745	(@mrobinson, @SimonSapin, #47745)	script: Rework DOM position comparison (#47745)
    ^ commit 0784072e4a60038666c7fc7c3adcb892ff20d3df
    # This change reworks the DOM position comparison function to be generic
    # over a `NoGcTraversal` trait. The ultimate goal here is to allow this
    # function to work in both the light dom and the flat tree (for
    # selections). In addition, to allowing a flat tree implementation, this
    # switches to `NoGC` approach which should dramatically decrease the
    # amount of rooting that happens during these comparisons.
    # 
    # The downside is that this required threading `NoGC` and `JSContext`
    # through a large number of methods and functions.
    # 
    # One small behavior change from this PR is that null nodes now have a
    # different abitrary order than non-null nodes (they now sort first).
    # 
    # Testing: This should not change observable behavior so should be covered by existing tests.
    #

    ^ commit 728cd59bdd78deec6e02c48e6536b2346e5c5559
    # In order to implement a lot of the behavior in `WindowProxy`, Servo
    # often needs to differentiate between and call shared methods on `Window`
    # and `DissimilarOriginWindow`. This change adds a

    # the `WindowProxy` behavior closer to the spec.
    # 
    # As far as behavior changes go, the movement here is minimal. There are
    # some minor spec compliance improvements around trying to fetch a frame
    # cross-origin via an index that is greater than the number of frames.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47651	(@Radeonares32, #47651)	paint: drop retired pipelines' scene state from WebRender on final exit (#47651)
    perf; memory leak on unloaded documents
    ^ commit 154139498ac6a6d705b9d2aa460e5704dcaf0552
    # Remove retired pipelines from WebRender once all pipeline exit sources have
    # completed. Servo already removes its local PipelineDetails at this point, but
    # the corresponding WebRender ScenePipeline was left in Scene.pipelines, causing
    # display-list memory to accumulate across navigations.
    # 
    # WebViewRenderer::pipeline_exited now reports when the final exit has completed,
    # and Painter sends a remove_pipeline transaction for that PipelineId.
    # 
    # Testing: No new automated test was added. I validated the change with repeated
    # top-level and iframe navigation, and with 10,000 about:blank reloads. Before
    # the change, webrender/display-list grew by about 576 bytes per navigation;
    # after the change it remained around 1.7 KiB throughout the run.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47630	(@mrobinson, #47630)	script: Inherit initiator origin when loading `about:blank` and `about:srcdoc` (#47630)
    dom; navigation conformance
    ^ commit fb160c4ca9acb14b49a998df0628225c77e631d3
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # The [specification] says that `about:blank` and `about:srcdoc` should
    # inherit their source origin. This means that instead of always creating
    # a new opaque origin in the parser for these kind of pages, we should try
    # harder to preserve the source origin.
    # 
    # [specification]: https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/#determining-the-origin
    #

    # <summary>Changelog</summary>

    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>Changelog</h1>
    # <h2>1.13.2</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Another bug having to do with macro expansion on <code>no_std</code> targets was fixed.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.13.1</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>fix bug where crate couldn't build for <code>alloc</code> without <code>std</code> too.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.13</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Adds <code>TinyVec::with_initial_len</code></li>
    # <li>fix the <code>Write</code> impl on <code>ArrayVec</code>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.12</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Add <code>schemars</code> support.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.11.1</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fix accidental OOM when deserializing hostile Borsh input.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.11</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>switched from <code>serde</code> dep to <code>serde_core</code> dep, which should allow more
    # parallelization in builds for downstream users.</li>
    # <li>add <code>as_mut_inner</code> method when the <code>latest_stable_rust</code> feature is enabled</li>
    # <li>add <code>is_full</code> method to check if the size is equal to the capacity</li>
    # <li>add <code>defmt</code> support</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.10</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Minimum rust version is now 1.55, and the non-const-generic impls are removed.
    # This reduces build times by over a second on average, which is
    # significant enough for a library crate.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.9</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Adds a <code>latest_stable_rust</code> cargo feature, which will automatically pull in
    # other cargo features related to the latest Stable version of rust.</li>
    # <li>Adds <code>ArrayVec::try_from_array_len</code></li>
    # <li>Adds <code>TinyVec::into_vec</code> and <code>TinyVec::into_boxed_slice</code></li>
    # <li>Adds support for <code>generic-array</code> crate</li>
    # <li>Adds support for the <code>borsh</code> crate</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.8.1</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/e00E">e00E</a> updated the rustc features so that they all
    # correctly depend on the lower version feature.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <!-- raw HTML omitted -->

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47058	(@jschwe, #47058)	threadboost: Enable threadaffinity boosting on linux (#47058)
    perf; threadboost
    ^ commit 6c680f538badaad8728b2199f16ae981b715ad9d
    # Follow-up to #47027.
    # 
    # The `ohos` code to boost thread affinity is based on linux `sysfs` and `sched_setattr`, so
    # it works on general linux systems. In the initial PR I intentionally did not enable it on linux,
    # to avoid any regression concerns in that PR.
    # This will only affect systems with a heterogenous architecture, so I think we should probably
    # give it a shot, since this could provide a significant performance boost on e.g. M1 macs with Linux
    # and is already implemented (this just moves the ohos code to a shared module).
    # Long-term we might also want to try different solutions like uclamp on Linux,
    # but that can happen in follow-ups.
    # 
    # This PR also doesn't implement priority boosting for Linux, since that would affect everyone, so that sounds
    # like more discussion / measurement etc. would be needed.
    # 
    # Testing: No performance testing, especially not on big.LITTLE hardware in CI.
    # 
    #

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47852	(@yezhizhen, #47852)	wpt: Make a few `touch-action` pointerevents tests work under 800x600 (#47852)
    ^ commit 21ee1ed675ea4758ae70b98fcf87d686e3990d34
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This should fix the WebDriver Out of Bound Error for Safari ([run](https://staging.wpt.fyi/results/pointerevents?diff&filter=ADC&run_id=5422356349583360&run_id=4684953817186304)), and prevent it from happening for Servo after https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47821. Right now Servo only FAIL but not ERROR as it ignores `touch-action: none` under certain circumstances (https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/47737) and scrolls the viewport.
    # 
    # Firefox/Chromium passed without the change as they don't always use 800x600 except for reftests. The approach we take is similar to https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/pull/48994.

    # The reason I open this in Servo is that it impacts https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47821.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47455	(@jschwe, #47455)	http_loader: Improve preallocation heuristic (#47455)
    perf; allocation
    ^ commit bbf86944f3143f9f43143631d55292e9a23913b6
    # Reduce the amount of intermediate reallocations by preallocating more generously.
    # if we don't have any information, we at least preallocate something. If the content is compressed and we know the compressed content-length, we pre-allocate 5x, which should cover typical compressions ratios.
    # For the http-cache, the backing allocation will be shrunk once everything is received (see #47454).
    # Also change the calculation in fetch.rs to prevent underflow, if we do call the trait wrongly.
    # On typical pages this seems to avoid ~90% of the reallocs during fetching and does not seem to negatively affect memory usage.
    # 
    # 
    # Testing: No observable change, covered by existing tests
    # 
    #

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47741	(@mrobinson, #47741)	script: Move live ranges to the `Document` (#47741)
    ^ commit 478bb535bc40e1b51e1a3345d4df1b338d9e8961
    # This change moves the live `Range` objects to be stored on the
    # `Document` itself. In addition to more closely matching the
    # specification, this allows avoiding another full subtree traversal
    # during `moveBefore`. In addition, this reduces rooting and
    # per-node-in-subtree operations during node removal.
    # 
    # Testing: This should not change observable behavior so should be covered by existing tests.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47891	(@kkoyung, #47891)	script: Partially implement "determine support" algorithm (#47891)
    dom; webaudio bugfixes
    ^ commit bac12f5565e0fa13cac2ca8c6f2baf3189350fd6
    # In the Modern Algorithms in WebCrypto API specification, the last few steps of the "check support for an algorithm" algorithm has been separated into the new "determine support form operation steps" algorithm, in order to improve the `supports()` method coverage for "encapsulate" and "decapsulate" operations.
    # 
    # We implement the new algorithm as a function, `determine_support_from_operation_steps`, of the `NormalizedAlgorithm` trait. When the `EncryptAlgorithm`, `DecryptAlgorithm`, `SignAlgorithm` and other similar types implement this trait, they implement the "determine support from operation steps" algorithm for the corresponding operations, separately.
    # 
    # This patch provides the necessary settings, and only move the steps for "encrypt" operation, as a proof of concept. We will move steps for other operations after this patch.
    # 
    # Specification of "check support for algorithm":
    # https://wicg.github.io/webcrypto-modern-algos/#dfn-check-support-for-algorithm
    # 
    # Specification of "determine support from operation steps":
    # https://wicg.github.io/webcrypto-modern-algos/#dfn-determine-support-from-operation-steps
    # 
    # Testing: Refactoring. Existing tests suffice.
    # Part-of: #47807

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/45481	(@sahvx655-wq, #45481)	block non-text/css responses for stylesheets under nosniff (#45481)
    ^ commit aa911418234bd747380a9ad310140507f6dd5899
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Fix the nosniff check for stylesheet loads. In `should_be_blocked_due_to_nosniff` the style branch used `mime_type.type_() != mime::TEXT && mime_type.subtype() != mime::CSS`, which only blocks when both parts are true. A `text/html` response still has type `text`, so the first part is false and the response is allowed, even with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` set. Per the fetch spec, anything that is not `text/css` should be blocked here, so the condition needs `||` instead of `&&`.
    # 
    # Testing: added `text/html` and `text/plain` style cases to `test_fetch_blocked_nosniff` in `components/net/tests/fetch.rs`. Both fail with the old condition and pass with the fix.
    # 
https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47803	(@mxmgorin, #47803)	paint: Do not panic when a rendering context has no surfman connection (#47803)
    ^ commit 3eb3d90c27fec59d8626cd478f244ec7c9223fac
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    # `Paint::register_rendering_context` calls `expect()` on `RenderingContext::connection()` and `Connection::create_adapter()`, so a `WebView` built on a rendering context without a surfman connection panics
    #  with "Failed to get connection". `connection()` is a defaulted trait method that returns `None` - an embedder that implements `RenderingContext` over its own GL context reaches that path; on devices whose drivers are EGL 1.4, surfman cannot create a `Connection`.
    # 
    # The connection and the adapter are only used by the WebGL, which  already treats a missing `PainterSurfmanDetails` as a recoverable error.  Neither the map nor this function is gated on the `webgl` feature, so a build  without WebGL panics as well. Both failures now log a warning and `PainterSurfmanDetailsMap::remove` no longer asserts that one existed.
    # 
    # Testing: New `test_create_webview_without_surfman_connection`. It panics  without  this change.

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47919	(@dependabot[bot], @dependabot[bot], #47919)	build: bump zstd-sys from 2.0.16+zstd.1.5.7 to 2.1.0+zstd.1.5.7 (#47919)
    ^ commit c3017e52c8e4d94ddd78d7e0645c85d3dbad9d9d
    # Bumps [zstd-sys](https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs) from 2.0.16+zstd.1.5.7 to 2.1.0+zstd.1.5.7.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/releases">zstd-sys's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>zstd-sys v2.1.0</h2>
    # <h3>Breaking</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>The <code>bindgen</code> feature is no longer enabled by default (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/pull/371">gyscos/zstd-rs#371</a>). The pre-generated bindings are used unless you opt in, so <code>libclang</code> is no longer needed for a default build. This makes hermetic and cross builds much easier (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/issues/370">gyscos/zstd-rs#370</a>).</li>
    # <li>The project is now licensed under BSD-3-Clause (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/pull/360">gyscos/zstd-rs#360</a>, <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/issues/314">gyscos/zstd-rs#314</a>).</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>New</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>cmake</code> feature: build the C library with zstd's own CMake files instead of <code>cc</code>, which handles cross-compilation better (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/issues/229">gyscos/zstd-rs#229</a>).</li>
    # <li><code>vendored</code> feature: always build the bundled C source, even when <code>pkg-config</code> is enabled, so a dependent crate can force a static build (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/pull/309">gyscos/zstd-rs#309</a>).</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>The wasm shim's <code>qsort</code> and <code>memcmp</code> are now <code>unsafe</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/pull/367">gyscos/zstd-rs#367</a>).</li>
    # <li>The x86-64 assembly is only built for x86_64 targets. It could never contribute code elsewhere - upstream gates it on <code>__x86_64__</code> - and some toolchains, wasm ones especially, cannot process <code>.S</code> input at all (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/pull/368">gyscos/zstd-rs#368</a>).</li>
    # <li><code>-fvisibility=hidden</code> is no longer passed to MSVC (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/issues/352">gyscos/zstd-rs#352</a>), and that is now decided from the target rather than the host, so cross-compiling to a <code>*-windows-msvc</code> target works too.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Commits</summary>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/commit/648acb476da66b4bb856043c9b8ea1fdbfe69093"><code>648acb4</code></a> Avoid let...else in the seekable callbacks</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/commit/e1152c1161fa6704b0b74bd538a9c92d2fe784a4"><code>e1152c1</code></a> Bump versions for the next release</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/gyscos/zstd-rs/commit/7caed6e87ae89a31524018d2b3da023f3e4e724e"><code>7caed6e</code></a> Derive the usual traits on ResetDirective</li>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47934	(@jdm, #47934)	script_bindings: Trace reflector objects. (#47934)
    dom; part of https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/39139
    ^ commit 967e455f78d5bb5575be0ecf50494867acd7f727
    # Ever since some of the earliest GC integration work in Servo (https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/1591), we have explicitly never traced the contents of Reflector as part of tracing a struct that contains it. Instead, any `Dom<T>` that is traced would trace the reflector of the contained type T. I can't remember why I made that choice and the reasoning is not documented anywhere; it's possible that it was intended as a performance optimization since there was no way to encounter a reflector during tracing without tracing it via a `Dom<T>`.
    # 
    # With the changes coming from #47749, this model no longer works. Promises stored inside traceable structures no longer have their own permanent roots, and so the current implementation silently ignores their reflector objects when tracing the containing structure. This leads to crashes when the GC frees the promises while they are still reachable from our Rust objects.
    # 
    # This change moves us to a model that is internally consistent—any reflector is traced when it is present in a structure, and we continue to trace the reflector of any object stored in a `Dom<T>`.
    # 
    # Testing: Existing test coverage must suffice. We do not have the ability to run with zealous GC in CI, since it takes too long on most tests.
    # Part of #47749
    # 
https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47895	(@mrobinson, #47895)	script: Add support for selections that span shadow roots (#47895)
    ^ commit 3f0dfbe95b0917752ddf587c168ebe42ba33dbea
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Allow selections to span shadow root boundaries when possible, based on
    # the tentative specification changes that define this behavior. The
    # general characteristics of this change:
    # 
    # - Boundary comparison is done by projecting the two boundaries into the
    #   same root and then comparing them, trying to consider deeper nodes as
    #   later ones.
    # - When updating from live range changes, collapse the selection if it
    #   would reverse the direction and also properly dissassociate selections
    #   when the live range root changes.
    # - Remove checks for whether boundary nodes are in a document tree (not in
    #   a shadow root) as other browsers aren't doing this and it causes
    #   tentative tests to fail. There's an open specification bug about this.
    # - Remove temporary same root checks that prevented cross-boundary
    #   selections.
    # 
    # Testing: This gets a number of WPT tests and subtests passing.
    # Fixes: #47117.
    #

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47950	(@dependabot[bot], @dependabot[bot], #47950)	build: bump aws-lc-rs from 1.18.0 to 1.18.1 (#47950)
    ^ commit 39c71518ccc14f49649d1c064e010a45662df5f7
    # Bumps [aws-lc-rs](https://github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs) from 1.18.0 to 1.18.1.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/releases">aws-lc-rs's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>aws-lc-rs v1.18.1</h2>
    # <h2>What's Changed</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Add ECDSA P-256 SHA-1 ASN.1 signature verification by <a href="https://github.com/assafvayner"><code>@​assafvayner</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1214">aws/aws-lc-rs#1214</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Adds <code>ECDSA_P256_SHA1_ASN1</code> for verifying ASN.1 DER-encoded ECDSA P-256 signatures over SHA-1, so applications can verify legacy signatures such as <a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/private-content-creating-signed-url-custom-policy.html">CloudFront signed URLs</a> using ECDSA P-256 with the default SHA-1 hash.</li>
    # <li>SHA-1 remains available for legacy verification only. This release does not add a corresponding signing algorithm.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Tighten AEAD, cipher IV, HKDF, ECDH, and RSA API contracts by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1215">aws/aws-lc-rs#1215</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>In-place AEAD sealing now verifies that <code>Extend</code> produced exactly enough space for the plaintext and authentication tag before passing the buffer to AWS-LC. Non-conforming custom buffers now return <code>Err(Unspecified)</code>.</li>
    # <li>Streaming cipher constructors now reject missing or mismatched IV contexts, matching the validation already performed by the one-shot APIs.</li>
    # <li><code>Salt::from(Okm)</code> now uses the output algorithm selected by the <code>Okm</code>, rather than the algorithm from the source PRK.</li>
    # <li>ECDH shared-secret storage is now zeroized on fallible derive paths.</li>
    # <li>RSA <code>verify_digest_sig</code> now requires the supplied digest to match the digest configured by <code>RsaParameters</code>, for both parsed and unparsed public keys.</li>
    # <li>Valid inputs are unaffected. Calls using inconsistent algorithms, IV contexts, or custom AEAD buffers now fail closed with <code>Err(Unspecified)</code>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Upstream AWS-LC</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>aws-lc-sys</code> v0.45.0 aligns with <a href="https://github.com/aws/aws-lc/releases/tag/v5.7.0">AWS-LC v5.7.0</a> (previously v5.5.0). See also the release notes for <a href="https://github.com/aws/aws-lc/releases/tag/v5.6.0">v5.6.0</a>.
    # <ul>
    # <li>v5.7.0 corrects <code>EVP_DecryptUpdate</code> for padded block ciphers so it modifies only the output range reported through <code>out_len</code>. aws-lc-rs now includes canary-based regression coverage around the documented minimum output-buffer sizes.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li><code>aws-lc-fips-sys</code> v0.14.2 moves to <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc/pull/3467">AWS-LC FIPS v4.2.0</a>.
    # <ul>
    # <li>Includes the equivalent <code>EVP_DecryptUpdate</code> correction for FIPS builds.</li>
    # <li>Restores FIPS builds with Clang 20 and newer.</li>
    # <li>Removes the FIPS compiler wrapper's dependency on <code>/usr/bin/env</code>, fixing builds in Nix and similar sandboxed environments.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Build Improvements</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Export <code>cargo:root</code> metadata when linking against a system-installed AWS-LC by <a href="https://github.com/weihanglo"><code>@​weihanglo</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1208">aws/aws-lc-rs#1208</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>The system-library path now exposes the installation prefix through <code>DEP_AWS_LC_*_ROOT</code>, consistently with the CC and CMake builders, so downstream build scripts can rely on the metadata regardless of how AWS-LC was built.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Fix <code>-Wa,--debug-prefix-map</code> handling with Clang and LTO by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1212">aws/aws-lc-rs#1212</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fixes Clang builds when <code>CFLAGS</code> contains <code>-flto</code> or <code>-flto=thin</code>. The assembler-specific flag is now used only with GCC; Clang's integrated assembler uses <code>-ffile-prefix-map</code> directly.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # <li>Filter raw target-triple <code>CFLAGS</code> spellings when compiling jitterentropy by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1207">aws/aws-lc-rs#1207</a>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Prevents inherited optimization flags from overriding jitterentropy's required <code>-O0</code>, including when environment variables are set by a parent process using raw or legacy-normalized target triples.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Issues Being Closed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>aws-lc-fips-sys</code> v0.14.0 fails to build with Clang newer than 19 -- <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/issues/1202">aws/aws-lc-rs#1202</a></li>
    # <li>FIPS compiler wrapper fails in Nix build environments -- <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/issues/1204">aws/aws-lc-rs#1204</a></li>
    # <li>Raw target-triple <code>CFLAGS</code> should be filtered for jitterentropy -- <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/issues/1205">aws/aws-lc-rs#1205</a></li>
    # <li>aws-lc-sys fails to build with Clang when <code>CFLAGS</code> includes <code>-flto</code> -- <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/issues/1211">aws/aws-lc-rs#1211</a></li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>Other Merged PRs</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Prepare aws-lc-fips-sys v0.14.1 by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1203">aws/aws-lc-rs#1203</a></li>
    # <li>fix(publish): verify packaged crate with cargo build instead of cargo test by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1201">aws/aws-lc-rs#1201</a></li>
    # <li>fix: address clippy::assert_is_empty lints in ECB cipher modes by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1210">aws/aws-lc-rs#1210</a></li>
    # <li>ci: replace removed llvm-devel-lite package in FreeBSD jobs by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1213">aws/aws-lc-rs#1213</a></li>
    # <li>Prepare aws-lc-sys v0.45.0 by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1220">aws/aws-lc-rs#1220</a></li>
    # <li>Prepare aws-lc-fips-sys v0.14.2 by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1221">aws/aws-lc-rs#1221</a></li>
    # <li>Prepare v1.18.1 by <a href="https://github.com/justsmth"><code>@​justsmth</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/aws/aws-lc-rs/pull/1224">aws/aws-lc-rs#1224</a></li>
    # </ul>
    # <!-- raw HTML omitted -->
    # </blockquote>
    # <p>... (truncated)</p>
    # </details>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47940	(@mrobinson, #47940)	script: Make `SharedWorker` tolerant of missing parent event loop (#47940)
    stability; crash fix
    ^ commit 7d43a2aaec5988fd6366c0d002c53c03d421129f
    # `SharedWorker`s are meant to outlive their parent event loop. Even
    # though `SharedWorker`s aren't complete enough to understand having
    # multiple owners, they shouldn't crash when their parent event loop goes
    # away. This change prevents that as well as ensuring that the
    # `WorkerGlobalScope` is always cleaned up even if a panic happens.
    # 
    # Note on testing: I tried very hard to make a test for this, but even
    # though I could get the worker to crash while the main page was still
    # alive, for some reason Servo's test harness did not detect the panic in
    # the worker thread.
    # 
    # Testing: See the note on testing above. This should fix flaky crashes in WPT tests.
    # Fixes: #47614.
    #

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47931	(@mrobinson, @SimonSapin, #47931)	script: Make visible selection updates flat tree aware (#47931)
    ^ commit c4fbc94828687598b66418bc8961acb97a3bb172
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This change reworks the visible selection flag update traversal to be
    # flat tree aware. In particular:
    # 
    #  - Selection boundaries that are not in the flat tree make the selection
    #    unrenderable.
    #  - Selection boundaries now may be inverted if their flat tree positions
    #    have a different order than their composed tree positions.
    #  - A new visible range is preserved before layout. This is consulted by
    #    layout to ensure that start and end boundaries are consistent with
    #    flat tree reality.
    #  - The traversal is refactored to be a bit more maintainable.
    # 
    # 
    # Testing: This causes a WPT test to start passing.
    # Fixes: #47120.
    # Fixes: #47121.
    #

    # When getting details of the origin (the origin and the internal
    # ancestor origin objects list), gracefully handle a missing parent. A
    # parent `Document` may be missing if it is in the process of shutting down and the
    # child hasn't ended yet. In addition, when getting both the parent origin
    # and the ancestor origin objects list, get both at the same time so that
    # they are both sampled at the same time and internally consistent. This
    # also reduces the amount of synchronous IPC.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47955	(@jdm, #47955)	script: Do not treat traced promise objects as GC roots. (#47955)
    dom; part of https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/39139
    ^ commit 0046833c767422d4251e9d1e2fca59e95b574a66
    # This change introduces actual behaviour differences between `Rc<Promise>`, `RootedPromise`, and `TracedPromise`. `Rc<Promise>`. Any `Rc<Promise>` returned from constructor methods on `Promise` is rooted. `RootedPromise` values are always rooted. `TracedPromise` values are never rooted. More subtly, `Rc<Promise>` values _extracted_ from either `RootedPromise` or `TracedPromise` are never rooted, so #47954 is required to avoid introducing a soundness hole.
    # 
    # Once #47747 is fixed we will be able to remove all Promise methods that return new `Rc<Promise>` objects, as well as the ability for a `Promise` instance to root itself. 
    # 
    # Testing: Existing tests suffice. I saw lots of crashes in WPT when I didn't get the rooting logic quite right while iterating on this code.
    # Fixes: #47749

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47742	(@SimonSapin, @mrobinson, #47742)	shared: Compact `Utf8CodeUnits` and friends (#47742)
    perf; less memory usage
    ^ commit a47d365c103b5363166dcd66512e9991b9f3ee73
    # Fixes https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/47696, attempt number 2
    # Closes https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47714, the previous attempt
    # Testing: existing WPT and unit tests
    # 
    # In that previous attempt I used `NonMaxU32` in order to optimize the size of `Option<Utf8CodeUnits>`. This made `u32::MAX` memory-unsafe to use, so as a precaution I made every operation checked, returning `Result`s. Unsurprisingly, this was a huge pain to deal with. I gave up before getting the `script` crate to compile.
    # 
    # ## Smaller `RangeAny`
    # 
    # Instead of the `Option` size optimization, per https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/47696#issuecomment-5499688316 I’ve changed `RangeAny` from storing two options (each with a 64-bit or 32-bit tag because of alignment) to a single 4-variant enum (with a single tag). So `RangeAny<u32>` occupies 12 bytes instead of 16. This change is internal, the public API is still based on two options (though no longer bare public fields)
    # 
    # ## `u32` overflow
    # 
    # In this PR I’ve still reduced the width of `Utf*CodeUnits` from `usize` to `u32` but left the full `u32` range available. `impl Add for Utf8CodeUnits` is still there (not replaced with `checked_add`) and still defers to plain integer addition: checked with panic on overflow when compiled in debug mode, or wrapping in release mode.
    # 
    # In either compilation mode, trying to deal with offsets greater than `u32::MAX` would be a bug (though not memory-unsafe). To help surface this issue to callers, I’ve changed some APIs from taking `&str` arguments to taking a new `struct Str32(&str)` marker type that says: “this string is expected to not be bigger than 4 GiB (`u32::MAX` bytes)”
    # 
    # In practice the strings come from a few places:
    # 
    # * `DOMString` in many places in the script crate: multi-representation, UTF-8 or Latin-1
    # * `Rope` for the text value of `<input>` or `<textarea>` elements: UTF-8 `Vec<String>` (currently does not cache its total length)
    # * Text value of `CharacterData` DOM nodes: UTF-8  `String`
    # * Rendered text in layout: mostly from DOM text (`CharacterData`) nodes, but for `InlineFormattingContext` also computes offsets spanning multiple DOM nodes
    # 
    # Maybe we could systematically enforce limits on their sizes (with e.g. JS exceptions instead of Rust panics or silently incorrect behavior), but I haven’t done so in this PR. The script crate was already using `u32` is some places, so I suspect there were already bugs and/or limitations with very large strings including [a limitation from SpiderMonkey](https://stackoverflow.com/a/65570725).
    # 
    # ## Splitting surrogate pairs
    # 
    # As a drive-by improvement, `Utf*CodeUnits` is used in more places instead of `usize`, and their methods instead of ad-hoc offset or length computations. The latter points out a subtle aspect of converting a UTF-16 offset to UTF-8 or UTF-32: if the UTF-16 offset would split a surrogate pair (for a non-BMP code point), `Utf16CodeUnits` methods used to arbitrarily snap to the code point boundary *after* the whole pair. When using this method to implement the `maxlength` HTML attribute of `<input>` or `<textarea>` however, the snap must be to *before* the surrogate pair. So I’ve changed the behavior of the `Utf16CodeUnits` methods, which also affects the result of changing the selection range through DOM APIs.

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48055	(@mrobinson, #48055)	constellation: Prevent subsequent messaging to crashed pipelines (#48055)
    ^ commit b607b2734776232ec7f0664140af25739448a16a
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    # When an `EventLoop` crashes it replaces its entire `Pipeline` hierarchy
    # with a new crash page, but it was not actually cleaning up the replaced
    # `Pipeline`s. This meant that they would leak after a crash. When a
    # pipeline is replaced in the `Constellation` it sends a message to [disable
    # accessibility] on the old pages. Since the pipeline this message sent to
    # has crashed, this triggers *another* crash which is blamed on the crash
    # page -- because `handle_send_error` and `handle_panic_in_webview` do not
    # track whether the crash corresponds to the currently active frame tree.
    # 
    # This change fixes that by cleaning up crashed pipelines in
    # `handle_panic` so that all future messaging to them will be
    # ignored instead of being sent and triggering a crash.
    # 
    # [disable accessibility]: https://github.com/servo/servo/blob/49475e051200df267fb8f9ffef810738464ed692/components/constellation/constellation.rs#L3214
    # 
    # Testing: This change adds a unit test.
    # Fixes: #47874.
    #

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48113	(@dependabot[bot], @dependabot[bot], #48113)	build: bump jiff-core from 0.1.0 to 0.1.1 (#48113)
    ^ commit 7e0f5be5563c20c8285f5a6f59ac7649d8253dbd
    # Bumps [jiff-core](https://github.com/BurntSushi/jiff) from 0.1.0 to 0.1.1.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/BurntSushi/jiff/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">jiff-core's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>0.1.19 (2025-01-02)</h1>
    # <p>This releases includes a UTF-8 related bug fix and a few enhancements.</p>
    # <p>Firstly, a <code>Span</code>'s default <code>Display</code> implementation now writes uppercase
    # unit designator labels. That means you'll get <code>P1Y2M3DT4H5M6S</code> instead
    # of <code>P1y2m3dT4h5m6s</code> by default. You can restore previous behavior via
    # <code>jiff::fmt::temporal::SpanPrinter::lowercase</code>. This change was made to improve
    # interoperability.</p>
    # <p>Secondly, <code>SignedDuration</code> now supports rounding via <code>SignedDuration::round</code>.
    # Note that it only supports rounding time units (hours or smaller). In order to
    # round with calendar units, you'll still need to use a <code>Span</code>.</p>
    # <p>Enhancements:</p>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/BurntSushi/jiff/issues/130">#130</a>:
    # Document value ranges for methods like <code>year</code>, <code>day</code>, <code>hour</code> and so on.</li>
    # <li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/BurntSushi/jiff/issues/187">#187</a>:
    # Add a rounding API (for time units only) on <code>SignedDuration</code>.</li>
    # <li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/BurntSushi/jiff/issues/190">#190</a>:
    # <code>Span</code> and <code>SignedDuration</code> now use uppercase unit designator labels in their
    # default ISO 8601 <code>Display</code> implementation.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <p>Bug fixes:</p>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/BurntSushi/jiff/issues/155">#155</a>:
    # Relax <code>strftime</code> format strings from ASCII-only to all of UTF-8.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h1>0.1.18 (2024-12-31)</h1>
    # <p>This release includes a few minor enhancements. Namely, the ability to iterate
    # over time zone transitions (in the future or the past), and some improvements
    # to failure modes when <code>Timestamp</code> and <code>Span</code> arithmetic fails.</p>
    # <p>Enhancements:</p>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/BurntSushi/jiff/issues/144">#144</a>:
    # Add APIs for iterating over the transitions of a time zone.</li>
    # <li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/BurntSushi/jiff/issues/145">#145</a>:
    # Improve docs and error messages around fallible <code>Timestamp</code> arithmetic.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h1>0.1.17 (2024-12-31)</h1>
    # <p>This release enhances Jiff's support for <code>no_std</code> environments by making its
    # <code>alloc</code> feature optional. When <code>alloc</code> is disabled, only fixed offset time
    # zones are supported and error messages are significantly degraded. If you have
    # core-only use cases for Jiff, I'd love to hear about them on the issue tracker.</p>
    # <!-- raw HTML omitted -->
    # </blockquote>
    # <p>... (truncated)</p>
    # </details>

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48118	(@dependabot[bot], @dependabot[bot], #48118)	build: bump bytemuck_derive from 1.12.0 to 1.12.1 (#48118)
    ^ commit dace27d9eb662fdb5d460302432013c5b8fc17ff
    # Bumps [bytemuck_derive](https://github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck) from 1.12.0 to 1.12.1.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck/blob/main/changelog.md">bytemuck_derive's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>1.12.1</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Patch bumped the required <code>bytemuck_derive</code> version because of a regression in
    # how it handled <code>align(N)</code> attributes.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.12</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>This minor version bump is caused by a version bump in our <code>bytemuck_derive</code>
    # dependency, which is in turn caused by a mixup in the minimum version of <code>syn</code>
    # that <code>bytemuck_derive</code> uses. See <a href="https://redirect.github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck/issues/122">Issue
    # 122</a>. There's not any
    # specific &quot;new&quot; API as you might normally expect from a minor version bump.</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/pali6">pali</a> fixed a problem with SPIR-V builds being
    # broken. The error handling functions were trying to be generic over <code>Display</code>,
    # which the error types normally support, except on SPIR-V targets (which run on
    # the GPU and don't have text formatting).</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.11</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/WaffleLapkin">WaffleLapkin</a> added <code>wrap_box</code> and <code>peel_box</code>
    # to the <code>TransparentWrapperAlloc</code> trait. Default impls of these functions are
    # provided, and (as usual with the transparent trait stuff) you should not override
    # the default versions.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.10</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/TheEdward162">TheEdward162</a> added the <code>ZeroableInOption</code>
    # and <code>PodInOption</code> traits. These are for types that are <code>Zeroable</code> or <code>Pod</code>
    # <em>when in an option</em>, but not on their own. We provide impls for the various
    # &quot;NonZeroINTEGER&quot; types in <code>core</code>, and if you need to newtype a NonZero value
    # then you can impl these traits when you use <code>repr(transparent)</code>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.9.1</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Bumped the minimum <code>bytemuck_derive</code> dependency version from <code>1.0</code> to <code>1.1</code>.
    # The fact that <code>bytemuck</code> and <code>bytemuck_derive</code> are separate crates at all is
    # an unfortunate technical limit of current Rust, woe and calamity.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>1.9.0</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/fu5ha">fu5ha</a> added the <code>NoUninit</code>, <code>AnyBitPattern</code>, and
    # <code>CheckedBitPattern</code> traits. This allows for a more fine-grained level of
    # detail in what casting operations are allowed for a type. Types that already
    # implement <code>Zeroable</code> and <code>Pod</code> will have a blanket impl for these new traits.
    # This is a &quot;preview&quot; of the direction that the crate will probably go in the
    # eventual 2.0 version. We're still waiting on <a href="https://github.com/rust-lang/project-safe-transmute">Project Safe
    # Transmute</a> for an actual
    # 2.0 version of the crate, but until then please enjoy this preview.</li>
    # <li>Also Fusha added better support for <code>union</code> types in the derive macros. I
    # still don't know how any of the proc-macro stuff works at all, so please</li>
    # </ul>
    # <!-- raw HTML omitted -->
    # </blockquote>
    # <p>... (truncated)</p>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Commits</summary>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck/commit/839ee557dd9b1ac5a0b0155cabba71f96b4ccd06"><code>839ee55</code></a> chore: Release bytemuck_derive version 1.12.1</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck/commit/d551f60c4f16b657159aff3c8a581c2e685b7c7d"><code>d551f60</code></a> changelog</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck/commit/9b27324673b75bce7047055ca22a512d1e7fec21"><code>9b27324</code></a> Forward struct where-clauses on TransparentWrapper derive (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck/issues/354">#354</a>)</li>
    # <li>See full diff in <a href="https://github.com/Lokathor/bytemuck/compare/bytemuck_derive-v1.12.0...bytemuck_derive-v1.12.1">compare view</a></li>

    ^ commit 9773ecea8727358c98d75743597c3ee235fa335d
    # This change ensures that visible selection rectangles and carets have a
    # consistent height:
    # 
    #  - Selection rectangles paint the entire height of the line box so the
    #    rectangle height should be consistent across the entire selected
    #    contents of the line.
    #  - Carets now paint the line gap height of the font metrics of the
    #    text fragment they are attached to.
    # 
    # From a high level, this behavior matches Chromium.
    # 
    # Testing: This change adds a Servo-specific WPT-style text.
    # Fixes: #36924.
    # Fixes: #47553.
    #

    # - New traits introduced are HTMLVideoTrait, GPUQueueTrait and EventTargetTrait.
    # - Additionally, WebGPUTaskSource along with WebGPUGlobalTrait::queue_webgpu_task_source should get extra attention.

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48066	(@yezhizhen, #48066)	webdriver: Dispatch element click and send keys via "dispatch a list of actions" (#48066)
    ^ commit 81eaaab09d5f85398aeff6ee81e2b7b0e0ab4c3b
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # For whatever reason, we were doing ["extract an action sequence"](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver/#dfn-extract-an-action-sequence) here which is only meant for [Perform Actions](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver/#perform-actions).
    # That is quite unnecessary as it goes through a series of redundant processing for element click/send keys, with side effect:
    # You got 3 ticks for element click instead of intended 1 tick.
    # 
    # This PR adds [dispatch a list of actions](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver/#dfn-dispatch-a-list-of-actions).
    # 
    # Testing: 
    # 1. Newly pass [focus/activeelement-after-nested-loses-focus.html](https://github.com/servo/servo/blob/0d552726d7f8ed33664bb29c922a06d1cba73fbb/tests/wpt/tests/focus/activeelement-after-nested-loses-focus.html).
    # 2. Added a wdspec test to make sure three actions happen in a single tick. Note that Chromium currently fail [`test_spin_event_loop`](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/d556bd72998c2a11556dc692a3e7c10347d1accc/webdriver/tests/classic/element_click/bubbling.py#L47-L51). So we manually wait 1 sec for event loop to spin enough time.

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/39923	(@mrobinson, #39923)	Do not animate hidden `WebView`s (#39923)
    ^ commit c974e3c7c795eb0a2d4b7382e8f447232f2c2b09
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    # When a `WebView` is hidden  it should not process animations. This
    # change makes it so that this does not happen by:
    # 
    # - Tracking visibility in the Constellation. Hidden WebViews are
    #   throttled and hiding and showing WebViews does not force Pipelines
    #   into a non-throttled state.
    # - Tracking visibility in the embedder-side `WebView` data structure.
    #   Hidden `WebView`s do not get new frame notifications and do not
    #   trigger animation ticks in the `RefreshDriver`.
    # 
    # Testing: This is a performance optimization so it is difficult to test this via
    # automated testing.
    #

    perf; less memory usage
    ^ commit 31e3c3501e181a67acc38a78e3f008d8e497c3c8
    # FontData is long-lived. In single-process mode the Vec is reused as the backing allocation, so it makes sense to shrink first, to return excess capacity.

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48124	(@mrobinson, #48124)	libservo: Wait for lcpEntries value to change in LCP unit tests (#48124)
    ^ commit 91e69cabe8a52c8bda1edd9fbeceea9015cdaabb
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    # This fixes flakiness in LCP (Largest Contentful Paint) unit tests.
    # 
    # Instead of assuming that `lcpEntries` will update immediately after
    # triggering a change, wait for the value to change. LCP updates happen
    # asynchronously so we cannot depend on things updating immediately. This
    # is already happening in some tests, but this change makes it happen in
    # all unit tests. The only downside here is that it is tricky to test that
    # the value *doesn't* update which some tests are asserting. If these
    # start to fail, they will fail flakily -- a pre-existing problem.
    # 
    # Testing: This is a fix for flaky unit tests (which typically are hard to detect
    # on CI due to the retry infrastructure). I verified this locally though by
    # running the LCP unit tests with `--stress-count 100`. Before these tests would
    # flake very often locally.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48127	(@Narfinger, #48127)	net: Use rustls native types for TlsSecurityInfo instead of converting to strings (#48127)
    perf; fewer allocations for each network request
    ^ commit 55c0e7698f72e0733b9628bf9a36dc7ac2a7d85c
    # This replaces the protocol_version and kea_named_group and cipher_suite in TlsSecurityInfo and related structs with the appropiate types from rustls with a small wrapper to facilitate serialization. For this we also include the new dependency serde_with.
    # 
    # As TlsSecurityInfo is constructed for every request with also string allocations, this reduces the amount of allocations. As this is also send over channels if devtools is enabled, it should be more efficient in this case too.
    # 
    # This might change the output of devtools information. Previously all these strings were implemented via Debug output which was not a stable API. We reimplement the Debug output for ServoProtocolVersion here. The other debug output is not changed.
    # 
    # Testing: I do not think there are automated tests for this.
    #

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48202	(@mrobinson, #48202)	libservo: Avoid double-borrow hazards when accessing `Servo` in `WebView` (#48202)
    ^ commit 07777aaa24af690a52648f1cd7e575659310cee8
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    # A double-borrow errow can happen when calling `self.inner().servo`, if
    # the call subsequently calls back into the `WebView` and tries to borrow
    # the inner data structure mutably. This recently happened more frequently
    # when the call to `WebView::hide()` called into `Paint` which later
    # accessed the `WebView` itself. This change fixes these errors in a
    # general sense by:
    # 
    # - Exposing a `WebView::servo` helper which clones the `Rc` `Servo`
    #   handle.
    # - Always accessing `Servo` via that clone.
    # 
    # Testing: This change adds a unit test for the error that uncovered this issue.
    # Fixes: #48197.
    #

    # This change makes it easier to understand the memory impact of reloading a page over and over (per #45262). https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48038	(@mrobinson, @yvt, #48038)	script: Cache cross-origin property holders in a slot (#48038)
    ^ commit 1f40286a9d0202b247c3a0a9637ead85dd7dabc4
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Cross-origin properties allow cross-origin access of properties on
    # exotic objects such as the `WindowProxy` and `ProxyObject`. The
    # specification says that these should be cached between accesses. This
    # change is a direct port of that caching from [Gecko] (which is license
    # compatible). Each exotic object stores a `WeakMap` in a reserved slot
    # that maps from per-realm key objects to holder objects that store the
    # cross-origin properties. During property access, the map and the
    # property holder are either created or fetched from the slot/map.
    # 
    # [Gecko]: https://searchfox.org/firefox-main/rev/39dcc5beb77ec138d9a3f3418fea1159efe54e2e/dom/base/MaybeCrossOriginObject.cpp#231
    # 
    # Testing: This causes some WPT tests to start passing.
    # Fixes: #28557.
    # Closes: #28559.
    #

    ^ commit 1f62ad96d9f52a7ae77a8721e0f99ff1aae30c49
    # Bumps [btleplug](https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug) from 0.13.0 to 0.13.1.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">btleplug's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>0.13.1 (2026-09-17)</h1>
    # <h2>Bugfixes</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Only request Coded PHY scanning on Windows when the adapter supports it.
    # Adapters without Coded PHY support (e.g. Bluetooth 4.x dongles) accepted the
    # request and started scans, but those scans never delivered a single
    # advertisement. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/473">#473</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix CoreBluetooth <code>subscribe()</code> never resolving when the remote device refuses
    # the notification request. Refused requests are now resolved with an error, and
    # overlapping subscribe and unsubscribe requests on one characteristic can no
    # longer be misrouted to each other. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/471">#471</a>)</li>
    # <li>Make <code>AdapterManager::add_peripheral()</code> idempotent, fixing a process abort when
    # two scan results for the same device raced on insertion. This was most likely
    # on Android, where rescans re-report devices already held by the shared adapter
    # manager. Duplicate insertions now keep the existing peripheral.</li>
    # <li><code>AdapterManager::add_peripheral()</code> now returns the canonical stored instance,
    # so a caller that loses a race against an existing peripheral receives the live
    # one instead of a fresh wrapper without its connection state.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Commits</summary>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/430e4d0bead3e859df17e0b8bfbc09b1ce9b6174"><code>430e4d0</code></a> build: Update dependencies</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/aa88ad0dd21f0d385627a36f8c4df1f9d08322cb"><code>aa88ad0</code></a> docs: bump CHANGELOG/version for 0.13.1</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/0c50923e79f6d58be4a9e1e30df5a4bb0fedada7"><code>0c50923</code></a> Return canonical peripheral instances from adapter insertion</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/79d0b69bb54ce876c1ec9d8c4b431dfa917ec256"><code>79d0b69</code></a> Make AdapterManager::add_peripheral idempotent</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/6f51bcbf68dfc5608f19760014ae2c224674adb2"><code>6f51bcb</code></a> fix: resolve refused CoreBluetooth notification requests</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/b8712360033d15006e70beb4485dd5ca6f64a157"><code>b871236</code></a> fix(winrtble): request Coded PHY scanning only when the adapter supports it</li>
    # <li>See full diff in <a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/compare/0.13.0...0.13.1">compare view</a></li>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48146	(@TimvdLippe, #48146)	script,webvtt: Implement `GetCueAsHTML` (#48146)
    wip dom; text tracks
    ^ commit cfa8441bd1afb00018f72e607670c7a4a3d3e9a4
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This implements the cue text parsing algorithm as well as the DOM construction
    # algorithm that maps the tree of WebVTT nodes to DOM nodes.
    # 
    # There are two commits, to make reviewing easier. The first commit only moves
    # code without any functional changes. That is to make it possible to implement
    # the relevant logic, as some of the functions in `webvtt/lib.rs` were also required.
    # 
    # The second commit implements the actual algorithms. It introduces an iterator
    # to make it possible to iterate through the tree of nodes. To make it possible
    # to move back to a parent, weak pointers are used for parents while strong
    # pointers are used for children. That way, the whole structure can be garbage
    # collected, but still traversed through.
    # 
    # Part of #22314
    # 
    # Testing: new WPT tests passing and multiple new unit tests

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48238	(@RichardTjokroutomo, #48238)	fonts: Lazily initialize `Font::metrics` (#48238)
    perf; faster font matching
    ^ commit 694133bbcf5ea3c3c9534f3b72df18bf2e088cc2
    # Currently, during font matching (`FontGroup::font_if_matches()`), we create the entire `Font` struct before doing the font predicate check. This is is wasteful, because the check itself only require the `handle` field, but we create everything including `metrics`. under the hood, `metrics` creation will call `FT_Load_Glyph` a few times.
    # 
    # To improve performance, do lazy initialization: the `metrics` field is initialized only if the code needs it (which is after the predicate check). This improves `FontGroup::find_by_codepoint`  from `29.68ms` to `10.95ms` on a test page on x86 Debian 13 (default settings). (Although latency is not a stable metric due to other processes running in the computer, so results may vary).
    # 
    # Testing: This only improves perf so is covered by existing WPT.

    # #47934 introduced a regression where objects could be GCed whose only pointer existed in a `Dom<T>` field of a new webidl object while its reflector is being created. https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48016	(@mxmgorin, #48016)	paint: Remove a closed WebView's pipelines from the WebRender scene (#48016)
    ^ commit 79eb20c158e61f42f25f66ac0be0e97d53a8fbba
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    # This sends `remove_pipeline` for each pipeline held by the `WebView` before its renderer is dropped. `WebViewInner::drop` calls `Paint::remove_webview`, dropping the `WebViewRenderer` that owns the pipelines map. The `PipelineExited` messages arrive when there is no `WebViewRenderer`, so `fully_exited` in `Painter::notify_pipeline_exited` is false and the `remove_pipeline` transaction is never sent. As a result, the pipelines' scene state is not released when the `WebView` is closed.
    # 
    # Testing: New `test_closing_a_webview_removes_its_pipelines_from_the_scene`. It fails without this change.

    # Bumps [rand](https://github.com/rust-random/rand) from 0.10.2 to 0.10.3.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rust-random/rand/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">rand's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>[0.10.3] — 2026-09-20</h2>
    # <h3>Fixes</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fix <code>WeightedIndex</code> panic when the sum of float weights is infinite; return <code>Error::Overflow</code> instead (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-random/rand/issues/1808">#1808</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix spurious <code>Error::NonFinite</code> from <code>Uniform::new_inclusive</code> on large finite float ranges such as <code>0.0..=f64::MAX</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-random/rand/issues/1821">#1821</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix possible panic due to sampling a deserialized <code>Uniform&lt;char&gt;</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-random/rand/issues/1831">#1831</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Changes</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Report exact remaining lengths from <code>WeightedIndex::weights()</code> and reduce overhead when reading weights (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-random/rand/issues/1838">#1838</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-random/rand/issues/1808">#1808</a>: <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-random/rand/pull/1808">rust-random/rand#1808</a>

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48371	(@TTyChud, #48371)	script: Use leaf-typed weak references for media elements (#48371)

    ^ commit 54f838fa178aaa81085b7f9e4c7bf9f162bd11af
    # Media elements are always allocated as an `HTMLAudioElement` or
    # `HTMLVideoElement`, but the weak references to them in `MediaSession` and
    # `HTMLMediaElementEventHandler` were created with the `HTMLMediaElement` parent
    # type, which is not the type their objects are allocated as. Add
    # `MediaElementWeakRef`, one weak reference per concrete media element type, and
    # use it in both places.

https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48364	(@mrobinson, #48364)	servo: Add support for system focus handling (#48364)
    ^ commit 15aa067b33ebaef0143420d857f3d36dc28705da
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Instead of letting web content decide whether or not a `WebView` is
    # "focused," have the embedder tell Servo whether or not the `WebView` has
    # system focus and separate it from the concept of the focused browsing
    # context. This makes Servo match the model that the specification
    # describes.
    # 
    # Now, when a `WebView` does not have system focus or a frame is not
    # active its selection highlight is drawn in a different color (hardcoded
    # for now). In addition, specification-compliant behavior is implemented
    # around system focus. This means that when a `Document` gains system
    # focus proper focus events are fired and `hasFocus` starts to return the
    # correct value.
    # 
    # This allows properly implementing the concept of the `WebView` "selected
    # for interaction" in WebDriver versus the one with system focus. We were
    # conflating the two before.
    # 
    # There are API changes here. Now instead of calling `focus()` or `blur()`
    # on the `WebView` embedders are supposed to inform the `WebView` when it
    # receives system focus.
    # 
    # Testing: This gets one new WPT test passing.
    # Fixes: #9482.
    # Fixes: #44308.
    # Fixes: #48254.
    #

    # script: Convert canvas blob, notification permission and custom element callbacks to use rooted/traced types.
    # 
    # Converts the canvas blob, notification permission and custom element
    # constructor callbacks (BlobCallback, NotificationPermissionCallback and
    # CustomElementConstructor) from `Rc<Callback>` to `RootedCallback`/
    # `TracedCallback`, and removes their `'rc': True` entries from Bindings.conf.
    # CustomElementDefinition, which owns the constructor callback, gains the crown
    # `must_root` and `allow_unrooted_in_rc` attributes because it is shared via Rc.
    # 
    # Testing: No behaviour change; existing tests suffice.
    # Part of #47889
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48409	(@TTyChud, #48409)	script: Only create the fetch body stream for fetch responses (#48409)
    dom; response api bugfix
    ^ commit 570fad1cc23685457e4fb89dcf0468dc9e2835c7
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # `Response::new` no longer creates a `FetchResponse` body stream: a JS-created `Response` starts with no body stream, and `Response::new_fetch_response` is the only way to get one, used by `fetch()`.
    # The old default gave every `Response` a stream fed by network chunks, which the static methods then kept, so `Response.error().body` and `Response.redirect(url).body` returned a stream instead of `null`.
    # Error and redirect responses now have a null body.
    # 
    # Testing: The nine subtests marked `expected: FAIL` under `fetch/api/response` now pass, with no other change in `fetch/api/response` or `fetch/api/body`.
    # Fixes: #29663
    #

    # These two implementations are unified in Gecko and we can do the same in
    # Servo. Internally, `Location` either stores a same-origin `Window` or a
    # `DissimilarOriginWindow`. This is the first step toward fixing
    # issue #26982. In addition to ensuring that both IDLs expose the same
    # interface, this fixes issues where `DissimilarOriginLocation` WebIDL
    # wasn't properly marking its cross-origin properties.
    # 
    # Testing: This causes several WPT subtests to start passing.
    # Fixes: This is part of #26982.
